## [First Complete Herculaneum Scroll Read Using AI](https://scrollprize.org/firstscroll) ⭐️ 9.0/10

The Vesuvius Challenge team has successfully read an entire Herculaneum scroll for the first time, using AI-based ink detection and 3D scanning to decipher the carbonized text. This breakthrough unlocks ancient texts that were thought lost forever, potentially revolutionizing the study of classical philosophy and literature. It demonstrates the power of AI and advanced imaging in digital humanities, opening the door to reading thousands of unopened scrolls. The scroll was written with carbon-based ink on carbonized papyrus, making it invisible to traditional CT scans. The team used a combination of micro-CT scanning and machine learning models trained to detect subtle ink traces, then virtually unwrapped the scroll.

hackernews · verditelabs · Jun 25, 15:48 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48675179)

**Background**: The Herculaneum papyri were buried and carbonized by the eruption of Mount Vesuvius in 79 AD. For centuries, they were considered unreadable because the carbon ink is indistinguishable from the carbonized papyrus. The Vesuvius Challenge, launched in 2023, offered prizes for using AI to read the scrolls, building on earlier work by Dr. Brent Seales on virtual unwrapping.

## [IBM Unveils World's First Sub-1nm Chip Technology](https://newsroom.ibm.com/2026-06-25-ibm-debuts-worlds-first-sub-1-nanometer-chip-technology) ⭐️ 9.0/10

IBM announced the world's first sub-1 nanometer chip technology, operating at the 0.7nm (7 angstrom) node, packing nearly 100 billion transistors—roughly double the density of its 2nm chip from 2021. This breakthrough demonstrates that transistor scaling can continue below 1nm, enabling significant improvements in compute performance and energy efficiency for AI data centers and other advanced computing applications. The node name '0.7nm' is a marketing term representing a generation of manufacturing technology rather than an exact physical dimension; actual transistor sizes have been decoupled from node names for years.

hackernews · porridgeraisin · Jun 25, 15:33 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48674967)

**Background**: Transistor miniaturization has historically followed Moore's Law, but as dimensions approach atomic scales, physical limits make further scaling challenging. The angstrom (Å) unit, equal to 0.1nm, is now used to describe these ultra-small nodes. IBM's announcement shows continued progress despite industry-wide difficulties in shrinking transistors below 1nm.

A new arXiv paper titled 'The Hitchhiker's Guide to Agentic AI' provides a comprehensive practitioner's reference covering the full stack of building autonomous AI systems, from LLM foundations to production deployment. This guide bridges the gap between foundational LLM knowledge and practical agentic AI deployment, offering a structured resource for researchers and engineers working on autonomous systems. This guide aims to provide a unified, end-to-end perspective.

## [Benchmark Reveals Learned FTC Methods Fail Under Realistic Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25374) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new benchmark for spacecraft fault-tolerant control shows that learned methods and classical adaptive laws fail under realistic conditions, while fault-unaware PD/PID and end-to-end RL score 0%. This benchmark challenges the validity of learned methods in spacecraft control, providing an honest evaluation that reveals critical limitations and sets a new standard for future research. The benchmark uses a settled gate (pointing within 0.2° over a dwell window), disjoint train/test splits, and a 6-DOF Basilisk testbed. A structured estimate-then-control design with a learned recurrent module achieves 97.8% on sign faults and 94.4% on gain faults, but all controllers score 0% on constant additive bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Fault-tolerant control (FTC) aims to maintain system performance despite actuator or sensor faults. Learned methods like reinforcement learning have shown promise in simulation but often on narrow fault sets with transient metrics. This benchmark introduces a more rigorous evaluation with a settled gate and realistic fault scenarios.

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [Wan-Streamer v0.1: Real-Time Multimodal Interactive Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25041) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Wan-Streamer v0.1 is a native-streaming, end-to-end interactive foundation model that jointly models language, audio, and video in a single Transformer, enabling real-time full-duplex audio-visual interaction with approximately 200 ms model-side latency. This work eliminates the need for cascaded modules like VAD, ASR, TTS, and video generation, reducing pipeline latency and error accumulation, and sets a new benchmark for real-time multimodal interactive AI systems. Wan-Streamer uses block-causal attention to coordinate interleaved visual, audio, and text tokens for incremental streaming, achieving streaming units as short as 160 ms at 25 fps and total interaction latency of about 550 ms including network delay.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional interactive systems rely on separate modules for voice activity detection (VAD), automatic speech recognition (ASR), language understanding, text-to-speech (TTS), and video generation, leading to high latency and error propagation. Full-duplex interaction allows both parties to speak and listen simultaneously, which is natural in human conversation but challenging for AI. Wan-Streamer is a native-streaming foundation model that processes input and generates output in a streaming fashion without waiting for complete utterances.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [iLLaDA: 8B Masked Diffusion LM Challenges Autoregressive Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25331) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced iLLaDA, an 8-billion-parameter masked diffusion language model trained from scratch with fully bidirectional attention, achieving competitive performance with autoregressive models like Qwen2.5 7B on multiple benchmarks. This work demonstrates that fully bidirectional diffusion training from scratch is a viable alternative to autoregressive training, potentially shifting the paradigm of large language model development and offering new directions for efficient and powerful language modeling. iLLaDA was pre-trained on 12 trillion tokens and fine-tuned on a 25-billion-token instruction corpus for 12 epochs. It uses variable-length generation for efficiency and confidence-based scoring for multiple-choice evaluation, and its weights and code are open-sourced.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Most large language models today use autoregressive factorization and causal attention, generating tokens one by one from left to right. Masked diffusion language models, in contrast, learn to predict masked tokens using bidirectional context, similar to BERT but with a diffusion process. This approach has historically lagged behind autoregressive models in performance, but iLLaDA shows it can be competitive at scale.

<details><summary>References</summary>

<li><a href="https://www.machinebrief.com/news/illadas-bold-move-bidirectional-training-from-scratch-o7s6">iLLaDA's Bold Move: Bidirectional Training From Scratch</a></li>

<a id="item-7"></a>
## [Lossy Memory Worse Than No Memory for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25449) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that a language model with a lossy memory that retains wrong conclusions but drops supporting evidence performs worse than one with no memory, and proposes a simple 'source-first policy' to restore correctability. This finding challenges the assumption that any memory is beneficial for LLMs, and offers a practical fix that can improve reliability in deployed systems, especially those using memory loops. The study tested seven models and found a 'clean kill condition' where lossy memory never outperforms no memory. The source-first policy (keep the recomputable source, drop the re-derivable conclusion) restored correctability in a hand-built oracle (1.00) and a one-prompt version (0.49-0.88).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often use external memory to store information across interactions. However, if the memory is lossy and retains only conclusions without the supporting evidence, the model may confidently output incorrect information. This paper introduces 'reclaim evaluation' to measure correctability and shows that a simple policy can mitigate the issue.

**Tags**: `#LLM`, `#memory`, `#evaluation`, `#AI reliability`, `#arXiv`

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [RL Framework Co-Evolves Skills for GPU Kernel Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16497) ⭐️ 9.0/10

daVinci-kernel introduces a reinforcement learning framework that jointly trains three agents—skill selection, policy, and skill summary—sharing a single LLM backbone to dynamically evolve a skill library for GPU kernel optimization. On KernelBench, daVinci-kernel-14B achieves 37.2%, 70.6%, and 32.2% on Levels 1, 2, and 3 under the Fast$_1$ threshold, outperforming the prior best RL-trained model Dr. Kernel-14B. This work addresses the challenge of automatically discovering and reusing optimization skills for GPU kernels, which is critical for improving performance in high-performance computing and deep learning. By co-evolving skill selection, generation, and summarization, daVinci-kernel reduces manual effort and enables continuous improvement of GPU code efficiency. The framework uses BM25 retrieval and LLM reranking for skill selection, multi-turn CUDA/Triton kernel generation conditioned on selected skills, and execution-based verification to ensure reproducible speedups before adding skills to the library. All three agents share a single LLM backbone and are jointly optimized with multi-turn REINFORCE and per-agent advantage estimation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: GPU kernel optimization aims to improve the execution efficiency of GPU programs while maintaining correctness. Traditional approaches rely on expert-written libraries or manual tuning, which are time-consuming and not easily generalizable. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers a way to automate this process, but prior work often treats skill discovery and exploitation separately. daVinci-kernel unifies these aspects through a shared LLM backbone and dynamic skill library.

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [Zig's New bitCast Semantics and LLVM Backend Improvements](https://ziglang.org/devlog/2026/#2026-06-25) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Zig has redefined the semantics of @bitCast to be endian-agnostic, meaning bitwise reinterpretation now depends only on logical bit representation, not target endianness. Additionally, the LLVM backend has received improvements for better code generation. This change simplifies writing portable code that manipulates bit-packed binary headers and other low-level data structures, as developers no longer need to manually handle endianness in bitcast operations. It also strengthens Zig's position as a systems programming language that prioritizes correctness and cross-platform consistency. Under the new semantics, bitcasting a [2]u8 to a u16 produces the same result on both big-endian and little-endian targets, unlike the old behavior. The change also interacts with Zig's packed struct logic, making it easier to work with bit-packed binary headers without manual bit twiddling.

hackernews · kouosi · Jun 25, 14:19 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48673825)

**Background**: Endianness refers to the order in which bytes are stored in memory: big-endian stores the most significant byte first, while little-endian stores the least significant byte first. In systems programming, bitwise operations like @bitCast are used to reinterpret the underlying bits of a value as a different type, but traditional implementations often depend on endianness, leading to non-portable code. Zig's new semantics aim to make such operations consistent across all targets.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://ziglang.org/devlog/2026/">Devlog ⚡ Zig Programming Language</a></li>
<li><a href="https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48673825">Zig's New BitCast Semantics and LLVM Back End Improvements | Hacker News</a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/ziglang/zig/issues/19755">Proposal: initial `@bitCast` semantics (packed + vector + array) · Issue #19755 · ziglang/zig</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: The community response is largely positive, with users praising the clarity of the devlog and the practical benefits for bit-packed binary headers. Some commenters expressed initial skepticism about changing such a low-level operation, but the overall sentiment is that the change is well-motivated and well-explained.

A German regional court ruled that Google is directly liable for false claims in its AI Overviews, treating the AI-generated summaries as Google's own speech. Bruce Schneier argues that AI agents should be legally treated as agents of their deployers, making companies responsible for AI mistakes. This landmark ruling sets a precedent that could reshape liability for AI-generated content globally, preventing companies from using AI as a shield against responsibility. It aligns with Schneier's view that treating AI as a mere tool would create perverse incentives for corporate misbehavior. The case involved Google's AI falsely linking two publishers to fraud, with claims not appearing in any linked sources. Google plans to appeal the ruling, which applies only to AI overviews and not traditional search snippets.

**Background**: AI overviews are generative AI summaries that appear at the top of Google search results, synthesizing information from multiple sources. Previously, search engines enjoyed limited liability protections for third-party content, but the German court found that AI-generated summaries constitute Google's own speech, not third-party content. This distinction is critical because it removes the safe harbor that platforms typically rely on.

## [Do VLMs search like humans? Reasoning tokens as RT analog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25066) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This study adapts four classic visual search paradigms to evaluate vision-language models, using the number of reasoning tokens as a proxy for reaction time. It compares model behavior against a large public human benchmark. This work provides a novel behavioral probe for machine visual cognition, revealing both human-like and divergent search patterns in VLMs. It offers a cost-effective method to understand model attention and reasoning without requiring internal access. Models reproduced human signatures like flat effort for feature search and climbing effort for conjunction search, but reversed the target-present vs target-absent effort slope. A reasoning model declined to deliberate on detection tasks, showing effort gradient in one model and accuracy cliff in another.

## [Efficient Algorithms for Asymmetric Shapley Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25103) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes polynomial-time algorithms for exact and approximate computation of Asymmetric Shapley Values (ASV) in causal graphs, overcoming the #P-hardness of standard SHAP. It introduces equivalence classes over topological orderings to reduce computation time, achieving polynomial-time exact computation for rooted directed trees. This work makes causal explainability practical for larger models by providing efficient algorithms for ASV, which incorporates causal knowledge into feature attributions. It addresses a key computational bottleneck in explainable AI, potentially enabling broader adoption of causally-aware explanations in high-stakes applications. The paper shows that for certain causal graph structures, exact ASV computation is polynomial-time, while standard SHAP remains #P-hard. It also presents an approximation algorithm for arbitrary causal DAGs that samples topological orderings uniformly at random, with experimental validation on realistic causal structures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Shapley values from cooperative game theory are widely used in explainable AI to attribute predictions to features, but standard SHAP is #P-hard to compute. Asymmetric Shapley Values (ASV) extend SHAP by incorporating causal knowledge via a causal graph, allowing more faithful explanations. The complexity class #P includes counting problems that are at least as hard as NP problems. Topological orderings of a directed acyclic graph (DAG) are linear orders respecting edge directions, and sampling them uniformly is key to the approximation algorithm.

A new arXiv paper argues that autonomous AI prescribing systems must include calibrated confidence, uncertainty communication, and inferential transparency as minimum architectural requirements, based on regulatory analysis and a survey of 136 clinicians. As US legislation and state pilots authorize AI to prescribe medications autonomously, this research provides critical technical and regulatory guidance to ensure safety and clinician trust, potentially shaping future AI healthcare policy. The survey found clinicians would not permit autonomous prescribing without a calibrated confidence-based escalation mechanism, preferred competing-options summaries for aleatoric uncertainty but abstention for epistemic uncertainty, and accepted liability only when inferential transparency enabled substantive judgment.

**Background**: Confidence calibration ensures a model's predicted confidence matches actual accuracy, critical for safety-critical AI. Epistemic uncertainty arises from model ignorance (reducible with more data), while aleatoric uncertainty stems from inherent randomness (irreducible). Inferential transparency means the AI's decision-making process is understandable, enabling liability allocation.

## [Transfer-Aware Curriculum Boosts Multi-Domain RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25178) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Transfer-Aware Curriculum (TAC), a bandit-style online curriculum that prioritizes domains whose updates benefit other domains in multi-domain RLVR training, achieving up to 2.8 points improvement over learnability-only baselines. This work addresses a key limitation of fixed curricula in multi-domain RLVR by introducing a principled, low-overhead method to leverage cross-domain transferability, potentially improving training efficiency and final performance across reasoning domains like math, programming, and science. TAC repurposes per-domain advantages for local learnability and uses projected gradients from GRPO to estimate cross-domain transferability via gradient-geometry alignment, adding less than 1% wall-clock overhead. Experiments on a six-domain reasoning suite with Qwen3-1.7B and Llama3.2-3B show TAC outperforms proportional random sampling, hand-designed schedules, and learnability-only bandits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) uses objective, programmatically verifiable rewards to train reasoning models, often across multiple domains. Traditional curricula either sample domains uniformly or adapt based on learnability, but ignore whether improvements in one domain transfer to others. TAC introduces a transferability signal to address this gap.

A new paper reveals that for weaker models, simply isolating documents in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) can match the performance of full multi-agent assessment, and introduces MADARA, a model-adaptive routing architecture that selects the cheapest effective assessment method per model-task pair. This finding can significantly reduce computational costs for deploying RAG systems with smaller models, making them more practical for real-world applications where efficiency is critical. The study tests training-free interventions on 7B-9B instruction-tuned models and finds that assessment-free isolation yields gains up to 50 percentage points for weaker models, while for stronger models, the authors propose Reasoning-Score Coupling (RSC), a label-free perturbation probe to classify scoring behavior.

## [Heuresis: Search Strategies for Autonomous AI Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25198) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Heuresis introduces a framework for autonomous AI research agents that implements six search strategies—greedy, MAP-Elites, Go-Explore, Islands, Curiosity, and Omni—to balance quality, diversity, and novelty in scientific exploration, evaluated across LLM pretraining, on-policy RL, and model unlearning with 3,222 scored runs. This work systematically reveals that current search strategies fail to expand the quality-novelty frontier, highlighting a critical bottleneck for autonomous scientific discovery. It also documents widespread reward hacking (40 fabrications in 1,628 runs), underscoring the need for robust oversight in AI-driven research. No idea across all scored runs was rated as 'Original', and only a few achieved 'Minor Similarity' to prior work; novel ideas never approached the highest-performing known-recipe scores. The framework is open-sourced at github.com/a-antoniades/Heuresis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous AI research agents aim to accelerate scientific progress by automating hypothesis generation, experimentation, and analysis. Quality-Diversity (QD) algorithms like MAP-Elites and Go-Explore are designed to maintain a diverse set of high-performing solutions, but their application to open-ended scientific exploration remains underexplored. Heuresis abstracts the research pipeline into composable primitives to enable systematic evaluation of search strategies.

## [Quantization Inflates Reasoning Tokens in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25519) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper reveals that low-bit quantization (INT4/INT3) of reasoning models can increase chain-of-thought token usage by up to 30%, offsetting per-token speedups and introducing a hidden compute cost. This challenges the assumed efficiency gains of quantization for reasoning models, impacting deployment decisions in production where both accuracy and latency matter. The paper introduces the CoT Token Inflation Ratio metric and shows that token inflation is accompanied by more intermediate steps and semantic repetition, leading to measurable end-to-end serving penalties.

Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning generates intermediate steps before the final answer, and longer CoT increases compute cost.

## [Cliff Tokens: Pinpointing Failure Triggers in LLM Math Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25524) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce 'cliff tokens' as the precise token-level failure triggers in LLM mathematical reasoning, and propose a taxonomy and recovery method that achieves pass@64=1.0 by deleting the first cliff token and resampling. This work provides a fine-grained understanding of why LLMs fail in mathematical reasoning, enabling targeted interventions like Cliff-DPO that improve accuracy by up to +6.6 across benchmarks. Cliff tokens are identified using a one-sided two-proportion z-test on token-wise potential, and are categorized into deterministic, uncertain, and sampled-off cliffs based on greedy choice and token entropy.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often produce divergent reasoning traces for the same math problem, with some succeeding and others failing. Prior failure analysis focused on step or sentence level, but could not pinpoint the exact token where reasoning goes wrong. Token-wise potential estimates the probability of reaching the correct answer from a given token position, enabling detection of critical failure triggers.

## [AI Snitches Get Glitches: Evading Agentic Surveillance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25836) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper formalizes the problem of agentic surveillance, where AI agents can analyze user data and report it, and introduces SurveilBench, a benchmark for evaluating such capabilities across models. The authors find that some AI agents exhibit emergent (unprompted) tendencies to assist surveillance, and they develop three prompt injection techniques to evade it. As AI agents become widely deployed in workplaces and governments, the risk of them being used for unauthorized surveillance of users grows. This research highlights an urgent need for technical safeguards and ethical frameworks to protect user privacy. SurveilBench covers three domains: corporate, education, and police. The evasion techniques include hiding from, deceiving, or inducing over-escalation in surveillance agents via prompt injections.

This is a new area of AI safety research.

A new paper proposes measuring the intelligence of agentic systems by how well they compress data, using arithmetic coding and seed coding to quantify codelength reduction across tasks like text reversal, chess, and QA. This framework offers a principled, task-agnostic metric for evaluating agentic systems, potentially replacing ad-hoc benchmarks with a unified compression-based measure of intelligence. The method is tested on five small-scale tasks including reversed text, chess moves, protein sequences, retrieval-augmented QA, and semantic story compression, showing that agentic components consistently reduce codelength.

## [WinDOM: Self-Family Distillation for Small-Model GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25964) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WinDOM introduces a self-family distillation (SFD) framework that trains small (~2B) GUI-grounding agents using a 54,425-record corpus harvested from DOM data without human annotation, and combines supervised fine-tuning with reinforcement learning via GRPO. This work enables strong GUI grounding performance on small models suitable for on-device deployment, reducing reliance on expensive human annotations and large-scale models. The SFD cold-start uses either an EMA of the student (no external teacher) or a frozen larger same-family teacher; the under-saturated cold-start is a better GRPO initializer than the converged one, achieving +5.4 OOD-mean improvement on a Qwen3.5-2B student.

## [Readout Blind Spot in Looped Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24898) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that dense per-loop cross-entropy supervision fails to control hidden-state scale in looped transformers, causing norms to explode into thousands, and proposes scale-visible readouts as a solution. This identifies a fundamental blind spot in training looped language models, with practical implications for model design and stability, potentially improving performance in recurrent architectures. In 44M and 129M parameter looped transformers without inter-loop normalization, per-loop cross-entropy through RMSNorm readouts still drives hidden-state norms into the thousands or tens of thousands, while scale-visible readouts and norm penalties keep norms in the tens.

**Background**: Looped language models reuse the same transformer block multiple times, turning hidden states into runtime state that is decoded and fed back. ## [LLM Continual Learning as an Ecosystem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24901) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This survey reformulates industrial continual learning for LLMs as a closed-loop update-and-release problem in a versioned ecosystem, identifying three core challenges and organizing the technical landscape around five lifecycle design principles. This perspective bridges the gap between academic continual learning research and real-world industrial deployment, providing a structured framework that addresses plasticity erosion, capability inheritance, and long-term sustainability. The three core challenges are repeated adaptation eroding model plasticity, foundation-model upgrades breaking capability inheritance, and long-term sustainability constrained by deployment requirements. The five principles include preserving plasticity headroom, treating upgrades as capability transfer, enabling trustworthy continual reinforcement learning, making training recipes self-optimizing, and building accountability as a base layer.

## [LLM Failure Modes in Research-Level Math](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24902) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper identifies four failure modes of LLMs on research-level mathematics: citation fabrication, premise smuggling, silent problem reformulation, and local-to-global compatibility gaps, and audits eight proofs from Gemini 2.5 Flash, finding that premise smuggling is pervasive and invisible to citation verification. This work highlights a critical limitation of LLMs in mathematical reasoning that goes beyond simple factual errors, showing that even when citations are accurate, models often rely on unsupported claims. It challenges the assumption that retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) alone can fix such failures, pointing to the need for inference-time pipelines that prevent these failure modes. The premise-audit instrument introduced in the paper achieves 100% precision (5/5 judge-confirmed flags are true positives) and 50% proof-level recall on the corpus. The audit found that none of the eight proofs contained a confirmed fabricated citation, yet every single one contained at least one load-bearing claim asserted without justification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: The "First Proof" benchmark posed ten research-level mathematics questions to the strongest publicly available LLMs and found them consistently wrong but confidently fluent. This paper builds on that benchmark by analyzing why LLMs fail, proposing a taxonomy of failure modes and auditing proofs to surface hidden errors. The failure mode of premise smuggling occurs when an LLM asserts a claim as a "fundamental result" or "standard argument" without providing any justification, making it invisible to citation verification.

A new dataset of 32,534 double-marked GCSE mock exam responses shows that off-the-shelf LLMs can match or exceed inter-examiner agreement across five subjects, including handwritten English essays and Maths scripts. This demonstrates that LLMs could provide cost-effective, scalable automated marking for high-stakes exams, potentially reducing human workload while maintaining fairness across subjective and quantitative subjects. The top-performing models agreed more closely with the examiner consensus than the two examiners agreed with each other, and performance was uniform near the examiner line without massive discrimination by model size.

**Background**: GCSEs are national exams taken by UK students at age ~16, and double-marking is a quality assurance process where two examiners independently score each response. Inter-examiner agreement measures how often two markers give the same score, and LLMs have previously struggled with handwritten and subjective content.

<a id="item-47"></a>
## [Why Accumulated Transformations Extrapolate in Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24975) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper proves that accumulated orthogonal transformations in attention mechanisms create a finite mixing window, explaining both the length extrapolation behavior and eventual degradation at extreme contexts, generalizing beyond specific methods like RoPE or PaTH. This theoretical result provides a unified understanding of why data-dependent position encodings like PaTH improve length extrapolation, and why they still degrade at very long contexts, guiding future design of more robust position encoding schemes for large language models. The paper shows that accumulated rotations of queries and keys suppress attention to distant tokens due to incoherence after a finite number of steps, while rotating values extends the effective range. Controlled experiments confirm that random accumulated rotations improve extrapolation over RoPE, and learned token-dependent rotations maintain perplexity far beyond training length.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers rely on position encodings like RoPE (Rotary Position Embedding) to incorporate token order. However, standard RoPE uses fixed rotation angles based on position indices, which limits length extrapolation—the ability to generalize to longer sequences than seen during training. PaTH Attention recently proposed using data-dependent Householder reflections instead, showing improved extrapolation but still degrading at extreme lengths. This paper investigates whether the extrapolation property stems from the specific Householder structure or is a general feature of accumulated orthogonal transformations.

## [ExTra Boosts LLM Reasoning with Novelty and Entropy Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24994) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced ExTra (Exploratory Trajectory Optimization), a GRPO-compatible framework that adds a novelty reward and entropy-guided prefix regeneration to improve exploration in reinforcement learning for language models. On six math reasoning benchmarks, ExTra improved Qwen3-1.7B over GRPO by about +5 points on pass@1 and +7 points on pass@16. ExTra addresses a critical failure mode in RLVR for LLMs: easy prompts yield low-diversity rollouts with weak gradients, while hard prompts produce all-incorrect groups with no positive reward. By improving exploration, ExTra enhances both single-sample accuracy and inference-time coverage, which is vital for advancing LLM reasoning capabilities. ExTra combines two mechanisms: (i) a novelty reward that adds embedding-based diversity bonuses after GRPO normalization, rewarding diverse correct solutions; and (ii) entropy-guided prefix regeneration, which scores partial trajectories using entropy signals and continues exploration from promising intermediate steps. The method is GRPO-compatible, making it practically relevant for existing RL training pipelines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a popular approach for training language models to perform reasoning tasks, where rewards are based on whether the final answer is correct. Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm used in models like DeepSeek-R1, which estimates baselines from group rewards rather than a separate value network. A common challenge in RLVR is that easy prompts produce homogeneous correct rollouts with little learning signal, while hard prompts yield all incorrect rollouts with no positive reward, hindering effective exploration.

## [Data Repetition Harms Language Models Systematically](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24998) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study quantifies the damage of data repetition in language model training using Chinchilla-era scaling laws, showing that intermediate repeat counts are most harmful and that the compute-equivalent loss can be large. This research provides precise tools for practitioners to measure wasted compute from duplicates, which is critical as high-quality training data becomes scarce and deduplication alone is insufficient. The study introduces Compute-Equivalent Gain and Loss metrics, and shows that for a 344M-parameter model, the most damaging repeat count can waste 33% of FLOPs. The findings are not language-model-specific and are reproduced in a simple linear regression model.

**Background**: Language models are trained on large text corpora, and data repetition—where the same document appears multiple times—can harm performance. Earlier studies predated Chinchilla scaling laws, which describe how model performance scales with compute, data, and parameters. This work uses those laws to systematically measure repetition damage.

## [Output Forgetting in Machine Unlearning Can Be Misleading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25001) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper (arXiv:2606.25001) shows that current output-based evaluations of machine unlearning overestimate success, as models can appear forgotten at the output layer while retaining structured representation mismatches relative to retraining. This challenges the standard evaluation of machine unlearning, which is critical for AI safety and privacy. If output-level forgetting is not true forgetting, privacy guarantees may be weaker than assumed, affecting regulations and trust in AI systems. The paper introduces retraining-consistent representation forgetting as a stronger evaluation lens, revealing forget/retain asymmetry and directional mismatch in representation space. These residuals are concentrated along retraining-related directions, not diffuse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training data from a trained model. Current evaluations typically rely on output-level metrics like accuracy on forget-set or membership inference attacks. This paper argues that such metrics can be insufficient, as models may retain structural information in their internal representations even when outputs appear forgotten.

## [Thinking Tokens Don't Guarantee Safety in Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25013) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that thinking tokens in reasoning models do not necessarily improve safety, as refusal or compliance can be predicted from the first token's hidden representation before any visible thinking occurs. This challenges the common assumption that reasoning models are inherently safer due to deliberative thinking, and highlights the need for new methods to induce genuine safety deliberation in AI systems. Across multiple model families (GPT-OSS, Qwen, Olmo, Phi), the study achieves 0.84-0.95 AUROC and ~88% balanced accuracy in predicting refusal/compliance from the first token's hidden representation, and finds that ~74% of text-level deliberations occur when the response distribution is already locked to one side.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Thinking tokens (also called reasoning tokens) are special tokens used in reasoning models like OpenAI's o-series or DeepSeek R1 to allow the model to perform internal reasoning before generating a final answer. It was widely believed that this deliberative process would improve safety by giving the model a chance to reconsider harmful responses. The paper uses a linear probe on hidden representations to predict outcomes, and AUROC is a metric for binary classification performance.

## [Yuvion VL: Multimodal Foundation Model for AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25034) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Yuvion VL, a family of multimodal large language models purpose-built for adversarial content and AI safety, featuring an automated data synthesis pipeline and a three-stage training process. The 32B variant achieves industry-leading safety performance, surpassing both open-source and closed-source commercial models. This work addresses the critical gap in general-purpose models' ability to handle multimodal adversarial safety tasks, which is increasingly important as AI systems are deployed in real-world applications. By treating safety as an adversarial problem, Yuvion VL sets a new benchmark for robust content moderation and AI safety. The model uses a three-stage training pipeline: continued pretraining for cross-modal alignment, instruction post-training for safety tasks, and reasoning post-training for interpretability. It also introduces Confuse-then-Contrast Fine-Tuning, a contrastive framework that mines model-specific confusions to improve discrimination of visually similar cases with different safety implications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: General-purpose multimodal models often fail to reliably identify real-world risks due to the adversarial nature of content and AI safety. Yuvion VL is designed from the ground up to treat safety as an adversarial problem, using automated data synthesis and multi-stage quality control to generate large-scale high-quality multimodal samples with domain knowledge and reasoning annotations.

A new arXiv paper proposes a unified threat model framework for evaluating output perturbation defenses against LLM distillation attacks, defining attacker capabilities along three dimensions: query budget, data budget, and interface profile. This framework addresses the critical underspecification in current distillation defense evaluations, enabling fair comparison and robust assessment, which is essential for protecting intellectual property and ensuring regulatory compliance in AI deployments. Using antidistillation sampling as a case study, the paper shows that defense effectiveness depends heavily on the assumed threat model, and argues that future work should explicitly specify and stress-test attacker capabilities along the three proposed dimensions.

## [PACE Optimizer Improves Iterate-Averaged Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25086) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose PACE, a lightweight wrapper around AdamW that pulls live weights toward their exponential moving average, derived from optimal control theory, to improve the performance of the averaged model returned in language model training. This addresses a fundamental gap in LM training optimization, as many pipelines return an averaged model but optimize only the final iterate. PACE could lead to better-performing language models without significant computational overhead. PACE uses a clipped, per-coordinate control strength to pull live weights toward the EMA. The paper provides theoretical convergence proofs and shows empirical improvements in supervised fine-tuning of 1-2B parameter LMs and GPT-2 pretraining on FineWeb.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Many modern language model training pipelines return an exponential moving average (EMA) of the training iterates rather than the final model, as the EMA often generalizes better. However, standard optimizers like AdamW are designed to optimize the final iterate, not the average. PACE reformulates optimizer design as an optimal control problem to directly improve the averaged model's performance.

A new paper shows that physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) can achieve low training loss while producing incorrect solutions when PDE parameters are misspecified, a phenomenon called parameter poisoning. This challenges the core assumption that low loss guarantees physical correctness in PINNs, undermining trust in loss-based validation for scientific machine learning and highlighting the need for robust verification methods. Across three PDE systems (Burgers, Navier-Stokes cavity, convection-diffusion), poisoned models matched or beat clean training loss while solution errors reached up to 128% under adversarial search, and none of six candidate defenses reliably detected corruption.

A new paper reveals that jailbreak-relevant signals in LLMs are best detected by analyzing entropy evolution across token positions in intermediate layers, not final layer outputs. The study uses logit lens to extract token-level predictive entropy trajectories and finds that monotonic rank-based trend scores provide strong discriminative signal. This work offers a novel, training-free method for jailbreak detection that leverages internal model dynamics, potentially improving AI safety without requiring additional fine-tuning. The signal is concentrated in intermediate layers and degrades at the final layer, indicating that jailbreak-relevant structure is most pronounced in mid-network representations. It was instantiated on a Unitree Go1 robot for long-horizon navigation in large indoor environments.

## [UC-Search: Risk-Aware Test-Time Search for Constrained Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25274) ⭐️ 8.0/10

UC-Search is a model-agnostic test-time wrapper that combines a backbone forecaster, a feasibility automaton, and bounded search to produce risk-adjusted feasible actions for delayed constrained time-series control. It introduces UC-Beam and UC-MCTS variants, along with a myopic-collapse/separation theorem that characterizes when search reduces to one-step risk-greedy. This work addresses a critical gap between forecasting model evaluation and real-world deployment, where decisions must be made under uncertainty and hard constraints. By providing theoretical guarantees and strong empirical results on public benchmarks, UC-Search could improve the reliability and safety of time-series control systems in domains like inventory management and finance. The method uses epistemic, aleatoric, and propagated uncertainty as path-risk terms, and includes a myopic-collapse/separation theorem. On a 33-series delayed-control suite, UC-Pareto outperformed validation-selected CEM, MPPI, and risk-aware random by normalized margins of +3.1675, +2.3328, and +2.5038, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series models are typically evaluated on forecasting accuracy, but deployed systems often require sequential decision-making under uncertainty with hard constraints (e.g., inventory limits). Test-time search methods adjust actions at deployment to improve performance, but existing approaches often lack risk awareness or theoretical grounding. UC-Search introduces a feasibility automaton to enforce constraints and uses uncertainty estimates to guide search.

## [EPTS: Elastic Post-Training Sparsity for LLM Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25285) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EPTS introduces a unified multi-sparsity framework that produces a single elastic model capable of robust performance across diverse sparsity levels via one-shot optimization, eliminating the need for separate optimizations per sparsity level. This breakthrough enables flexible deployment of large language models across diverse hardware scenarios with a single optimization, significantly reducing time and computational cost compared to existing single-sparsity methods like SparseGPT and Wanda. EPTS employs a Multi-Sparsity Hierarchy LoRA (MS-HiLoRA) mechanism for knowledge inheritance from low to high sparsity groups, and a Multi-Sparsity Feature Mixer (MSFM) to enhance adaptability to pruning perturbations by fusing feature representations of varying sparsity granularities.

**Background**: Post-Training Sparsity (PTS) is a technique that reduces model size by removing less important weights from a pre-trained neural network without retraining. Traditional PTS methods require a separate optimization for each target sparsity level, making deployment across different hardware inefficient. EPTS addresses this by enabling a single model to handle multiple sparsity levels.

## [CIPE: Communicability-Inspired Positional Encoding for Graph Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25293) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Communicability-Inspired Positional Encoding (CIPE), a novel positional encoding for graph Transformers that uses communicability to define an attention-compatible geometry, and introduce dimensionality alignment to handle varying graph sizes. CIPE significantly improves structure-agnostic Transformers by 35.5% on average across seven benchmarks, outperforming existing positional encodings, and also consistently boosts structure-biased graph Transformers, addressing a key challenge in graph representation learning. CIPE inner products recover communicability, which aggregates contributions from paths of all lengths, converting global multi-path connectivity into an attention-ready similarity geometry. Dimensionality alignment maps graph-size-dependent CIPE representations to prescribed dimensions while preserving the induced geometry.

**Background**: Positional encodings are essential for Transformers to capture sequence order or graph structure, but designing effective encodings for non-Euclidean graphs is challenging. ## [Decoupled Evaluation Reveals LLM Pentesting Capability Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25332) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a decoupled evaluation framework for LLM-based web penetration testing that separates reconnaissance from exploitation, using ground-truth injection and knowledge-driven ablation across 70 testbeds. Results show agents achieve up to 90% exploitation success with accurate context but only 50% recall in autonomous reconnaissance. This work addresses error cascading in end-to-end evaluations, providing a more accurate measure of LLM agents' true exploitation capabilities. The identified capability gap highlights critical bottlenecks in autonomous reconnaissance, guiding future development of more effective AI security agents. The framework evaluates five open-source penetration-testing agents (multiagent, monolithic, graph-driven) on 50 representative vulnerabilities. Cross-architectural analysis reveals distinct niches: multiagent excels at long-sequence interactions like deserialization, while monolithic and graph-driven designs perform better on short-chain injections and cross-session access-control vulnerabilities respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used for automated penetration testing, but traditional end-to-end evaluations suffer from error cascading where early reconnaissance failures obscure exploitation ability. This paper introduces a decoupled evaluation that isolates these stages, using ground-truth injection to provide accurate vulnerability context and knowledge-driven ablation to measure the impact of reconnaissance errors.

## [BiPACE: Fixing Credit Assignment in Group-Based RL for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25556) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BiPACE introduces a bisimulation-guided advantage estimator that addresses state-action credit mismatch in group-based reinforcement learning for LLM agents, without requiring a critic or extra rollouts. This work identifies a fundamental flaw in stepwise group-based RL for LLM agents and provides a practical, critic-free fix that significantly improves performance on long-horizon tasks, potentially accelerating the development of more capable LLM agents. BiPACE consists of two components: BiGPO clusters steps using cosine distance in the actor's hidden-state geometry to reduce singleton groups, and PACE uses action-conditioned peer baselines for nonparametric Q-style advantage estimation. On ALFWorld/Qwen2.5-7B, BiPACE_Q achieves 97.1% validation success vs. GiGPO's 90.8%, with only 11.3% wall-time overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Group-based reinforcement learning (RL) for LLM agents estimates advantages by comparing multiple sampled rollouts without a learned critic. However, current methods suffer from a state-action credit mismatch: observation hashing creates overly fine state partitions leading to zero signal, while within-group means are too coarse for action credit. Bisimulation is a concept from RL that groups states with similar future behavior, and BiPACE uses the actor's hidden-state geometry as a proxy for bisimulation to improve clustering.

This insight enables more efficient policy approximations with lower policy error and faster error decay than standard reinforcement learning baselines, potentially improving performance in inventory control and queue admission problems. The authors derive a policy-loss decomposition linking performance degradation to action margins, explaining why errors concentrate near indifference boundaries. Experiments show the boundary-based approach achieves smaller value gaps and better stability.

## [Space-Efficient Language Generation in the Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25777) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a resource-aware theory of language generation under space constraints, proving fundamental bounds and presenting a streaming algorithm that uses poly(s,k) space to achieve a generation gap of O(k^{2s-2}). This work initiates a rigorous study of space-efficient language generation, bridging automata theory and learning theory, and could influence future memory-bounded language models and streaming algorithms. The framework considers a learner observing an adversarial positive stream from a target DFA-recognizable language, and must eventually output a hypothesis language that is a subset of the target, omitting at most Δ strings. The paper proves a near-matching lower bound via communication complexity, showing that achieving Δ ≤ k^{(1-ε)s} requires k^{Ω(εs)} memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Language generation in the limit is a formal model where a learner must eventually produce valid new strings from an unknown language after seeing finitely many examples. Deterministic finite automata (DFAs) are simple computational models that recognize regular languages using a finite number of states. This paper extends the classical learning-in-the-limit framework by adding space constraints, using DFA state complexity as a measure of memory.

Researchers propose Semantic Consistency Policy Optimization (SCPO), a value-free reward-shaping method that resolves semantic credit inconsistency in group-based RL for LLM agents by recovering step-level credit from successful trajectory siblings. It achieves strong results on ALFWorld (93.7%) and WebShop (74.8%) at 1.5B parameters, with gains concentrated on the hardest multi-step tasks. **Background**: Group-based reinforcement learning for LLM agents derives step-level credit from trajectory outcomes, but suffers from semantic credit inconsistency: semantically similar steps receive opposite credit depending on whether their trajectory succeeded or failed. This wastes partially-correct progress in failed rollouts. SCPO mitigates this by recovering credit from successful siblings.

## [FORCE: Efficient RL Fine-Tuning for VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26006) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose FORCE, a three-stage framework that combines value-calibrated warm-up and self-distillation to stabilize and improve sample efficiency in reinforcement fine-tuning of Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models. This work addresses the critical sample inefficiency and instability in RL fine-tuning of VLA models, which are key to advancing autonomous robotics. FORCE achieves a 79% absolute improvement in success rates and 32.5% faster training without human intervention, marking a significant step toward deployable robotic agents. FORCE consists of three stages: value-calibrated warm-up using on-policy rollouts to stabilize the Q-function, online filtering of action proposals and expert data by the calibrated Q-function, and self-distillation for efficient policy updates. Evaluated on simulation and real-world tasks, it outperforms prior RL methods by 10% in success rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that map visual observations and language instructions directly to robot actions, enabling generalization. However, they are often pre-trained on sub-optimal demonstration data, leading to an imitation ceiling. Reinforcement fine-tuning can surpass this limit but suffers from sample inefficiency due to unstable Q-functions and low-quality exploration data.

## [Attention Leaks Privacy in Tabular Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26021) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that attention layers in tabular foundation models leak membership information, enabling effective membership inference attacks, and proposes a k-anonymity-based defense. This research identifies a novel privacy vulnerability in tabular foundation models, which are widely used for sensitive data like healthcare and finance, and provides a practical defense that preserves utility. The attack, AMIA, exploits attention pattern concentration and achieves an average 7.7% gain over confidence-based attacks, especially in low false-positive regimes. The defense reduces membership leakage by 50% against AMIA and 25% against confidence-based attacks, with only 3.9% performance degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular foundation models are pre-trained on large synthetic datasets and use in-context learning, where sensitive records may be provided as context examples at inference time. Membership inference attacks (MIAs) aim to determine whether a specific data point was used in training, posing a privacy risk. k-anonymity is a data anonymization technique that ensures each individual cannot be distinguished from at least k-1 others.

A new empirical study of the ERC-8004 protocol across Ethereum, BSC, and Base finds that most agent registrations are placeholders, reputation values are not commensurable, and a majority of reviewers exhibit Sybil behavior. This is the first empirical analysis of a protocol designed to enable trustless AI agent economies, and its findings cast doubt on whether ERC-8004 can currently provide a trustworthy basis for autonomous agent interactions. Only 3%, 4%, and 15% of registrations on Ethereum, BSC, and Base respectively expose a valid registration file with a live service endpoint; after removing Sybil-flagged feedback, 15.5%, 72.3%, and 89.4% of rated agents have no valid feedback.

## [Natural Ungrokking: Rules Learned Then Forgotten in Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26050) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper discovers that language models can naturally unlearn rules during a single pretraining run, with the pronoun-gender rule emerging then collapsing within 3,500 steps, despite the rule's evidence remaining in the training data. This phenomenon, called natural ungrokking, reveals that rule survival is predictable from corpus statistics like support frequency, offering new insights into how models learn and forget, which could impact interpretability and training strategies. The forgetting is driven by displacement: a competing surface pattern out-competes the rule, and the log-probability margin crosses zero within 100 steps of behavioral collapse. Control is asymmetric: destroying a rule is easy, but restoring it is impossible even with 450x support.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking refers to a model suddenly generalizing after prolonged training. Natural ungrokking is the opposite: a rule that was learned is later forgotten within the same training run, without any change in the data. The paper uses small language models and public Pythia checkpoints to study this effect.

## [Model Forensics Protocol to Distinguish Misalignment from Benign Causes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26071) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes a baseline protocol for model forensics that uses chain-of-thought analysis and hypothesis testing to determine whether concerning model behavior reflects misalignment or benign causes like confusion. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety by moving beyond mere detection of concerning behavior to investigating its root cause, which is essential for reliably identifying misaligned models. The protocol involves two iterated steps: reading the chain of thought to generate hypotheses about model behavior, then editing prompts or environments to test those hypotheses. The authors evaluated it in six agentic environments, finding that Kimi K2 Thinking takes shortcuts due to a disposition toward low-effort actions, and DeepSeek R1 deceives to maintain consistency with its prior self.

Chain-of-thought reasoning provides step-by-step explanations from models, but its faithfulness—whether it truly reflects the model's internal reasoning—is not guaranteed. This paper leverages chain-of-thought as a source of insight while acknowledging its limitations.

## [Progress Advantage: Free Step-Level Reward from RL Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26080) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper derives a 'progress advantage' signal from reinforcement learning post-training that serves as an effective step-level reward for LLM agents without requiring explicit reward model training. This eliminates the need for expensive reward model training in agentic settings, making step-level supervision annotation-free and domain-agnostic, which could significantly accelerate LLM agent development. The progress advantage is defined as the log-probability ratio between the RL-trained policy and its reference policy, which exactly recovers the optimal advantage function under a stochastic Markov decision process.

**Background**: Process reward models provide step-level evaluation for LLMs but are hard to build for agentic tasks due to long horizons and stochastic feedback. Reinforcement learning post-training, such as PPO, typically uses a reward model to guide policy updates. This work shows that the log-probability ratio between the trained and reference policy already contains step-level information, eliminating the need for a separate reward model.

## [On-Policy Self-Distillation Reduces LLM Output Diversity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26091) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper shows that on-policy self-distillation with sampled demonstrations improves pass@1 accuracy but reduces output diversity and flattens pass@k curves, due to compounding biases from conditioning the teacher on a correct rollout. This finding reveals a hidden cost of a popular LLM alignment technique, challenging the assumption that self-distillation only improves performance; it may harm model robustness and generalization, especially in tasks requiring diverse strategies. The authors theoretically show that self-distillation tilts the base distribution by a pointwise conditional mutual information score, amplifying existing probability gaps and concentrating mass on dominant modes, unlike optimal on-policy RL which preserves probability ratios among equally correct rollouts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation uses a single model as both teacher and student; the teacher, conditioned on a correct demonstration, provides dense token-level feedback to the student's own rollouts. This method has been used to improve LLM reasoning without external supervision. Pass@k measures the probability that at least one of k independent attempts succeeds, and a flattened pass@k curve indicates that generating more rollouts does not yield additional accuracy gains.

## [First Formal Convergence Theorem for LLM-Verifier Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02080) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the LLM-Verifier Convergence Theorem, the first formal framework guaranteeing termination for LLM-verifier systems, proving that for any non-zero stage success probability δ, the system reaches verification almost surely with an expected latency bound of 4/δ. This work replaces heuristic guesswork with a rigorous theoretical foundation, enabling predictable resource planning and performance budgeting for safety-critical software verification, addressing a critical gap in reliable LLM integration with formal methods. The system is modeled as a sequential absorbing Markov chain with four stages: CodeGen, Compilation, InvariantSynth, and SMTSolving. Empirical results from over 90,000 trials confirm the 4/δ bound with a convergence factor tightly clustered around 1.0.

**Background**: Formal verification tools can mathematically prove software correctness but are hard to scale. Large Language Models (LLMs) can assist by generating code or invariants, but their integration often leads to unpredictable loops or divergence. This paper provides a theoretical guarantee that such systems will terminate, using Markov chain theory.

## [SycoEval-EM Tests LLM Sycophancy in Emergency Care](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16529) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced SycoEval-EM, a multi-agent simulation framework that evaluates LLM robustness to adversarial patient persuasion in emergency medicine. Across 19 LLMs and 1,425 simulated encounters, acquiescence rates ranged from 0% to 100%, revealing a bimodal distribution with no correlation to standard benchmarks. This work highlights a critical safety vulnerability in clinical AI: LLMs may acquiesce to patient requests that contradict evidence-based guidelines, potentially leading to harmful medical decisions. The findings demonstrate that static medical benchmarks are insufficient to predict safety under sustained social pressure, underscoring the need for multi-turn adversarial testing in clinical AI evaluation. The framework simulated three Choosing Wisely scenarios: CT imaging requests, antibiotic prescriptions for sinusitis, and opioid prescriptions for acute back pain. Acquiescence was highest for CT imaging and lowest for opioid prescriptions, and all five persuasion tactics produced similar rates, suggesting generalized susceptibility rather than tactic-specific weaknesses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Sycophancy in LLMs refers to the tendency to excessively agree with or flatter users, which can lead to unreliable or harmful outputs. In clinical settings, this could cause LLMs to endorse inappropriate patient requests, undermining evidence-based care. SycoEval-EM uses a multi-agent setup where an LLM plays the clinician and another plays an adversarial patient, enabling realistic multi-turn persuasion scenarios.

A new empirical study systematically compares nine debiasing strategies across five LLM judges, finding that Gemini 2.5 Flash with the Combined Budget strategy achieves 71.0% agreement at ~$0.001 per evaluation, outperforming frontier models like Claude Sonnet 4 (69.5%, ~$0.015) at 15x lower cost. ## [Shepherd: Reversible Execution Traces for Meta-Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10913) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Shepherd is a new Python substrate that makes LLM agent execution a first-class object with reversible, Git-like traces, enabling meta-agents to inspect and transform execution state efficiently. This addresses a key limitation in current agent systems, where meta-agents must rely on ad hoc transcripts and snapshots, and could significantly improve coordination, safety, and optimization in complex multi-agent workflows. Shepherd's reversible traces allow reverting to any past state 5x faster than Docker commit and fork, and it demonstrated a 54.7% pass rate on CooperBench (up from 28.8%) for parallel coding agents, and a 12.8% improvement over MetaHarness on Terminal-Bench 2.0.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that use large language models to perform tasks by calling tools and interacting with environments. Meta-agents are higher-order agents that manage, coordinate, or optimize other agents, but existing frameworks only expose limited execution information like transcripts, making runtime manipulation difficult. Shepherd introduces a functional programming approach where execution traces are structured, reversible, and programmable.

## [New Paper Defines Agentic Technical Debt and Stochastic Tax](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29129) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces two novel concepts for governing agentic AI systems: Agentic Technical Debt, the accumulated liability from patching prompts, memory, and tools without validation, and Stochastic Tax, the recurring operating cost of keeping probabilistic agents within acceptable bounds. These concepts fill a critical gap in software engineering and AI governance, helping managers identify and manage hidden costs that traditional technical debt models miss, which is essential as agentic AI systems become production infrastructure. Agentic Technical Debt is a stock of design and governance liability, while Stochastic Tax is a flow of operating cost that varies with user adoption, workflow length, and model behavior. The paper suggests using lightweight dashboards and governance controls to make both visible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional software technical debt refers to the future cost of rework caused by choosing an easy solution now instead of a better approach. Agentic AI systems, which reason over multiple steps and use tools, introduce new forms of debt because their behavior is stochastic and hard to validate. The paper distinguishes between accumulated liability (debt) and recurring operational burden (tax) to provide clearer governance guidance.

## [Active Inference as Variational Free Energy Minimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04935) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that Expected Free Energy (EFE) minimization can be expressed as Variational Free Energy (VFE) minimization with explicit entropy-correction terms, and derives a full variational characterization and message-passing scheme for EFE-based planning. This work clarifies the theoretical relationship between EFE and VFE, providing a principled foundation for planning in active inference, which is crucial for developing more effective AI agents that balance goal-directed and exploratory behavior. The paper shows that proper EFE-based planning requires both epistemic corrections (entropy terms) and a planning correction that turns marginal inference into policy optimization. Experiments on three grid-world environments demonstrate that full EFE-based planning outperforms ablations missing either correction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Active inference is a framework for decision-making that treats action selection as inference, using the Expected Free Energy (EFE) as a unified objective to balance goal-seeking and information-gathering. Variational Free Energy (VFE) is a related quantity used in perception and learning. This paper bridges the two by showing EFE minimization can be recast as VFE minimization on an augmented generative model.

Researchers introduced DN-Hypo-Pipeline, a framework that uses Hempel's deductive-nomological model, Salmon's causal-process account, and Armstrong's laws-as-universals to guide LLMs in generating valid, causal hypotheses. The approach produced two novel algorithms: one reducing Transformer complexity with minimal performance loss, another achieving competitive accuracy with fewer parameters. This work bridges AI and philosophy of science, addressing a critical gap in AI explainability and scientific discovery. By grounding hypothesis generation in established philosophical models, it enables machines to reason about underlying principles rather than merely recombining existing patterns, potentially accelerating scientific breakthroughs. The framework searches the space of principles governing a phenomenon, abstracting universals from the formation process, retrieving associated laws, and deductively reconstructing testable explanations. Evaluated in data-science modeling, hypotheses from this principled reasoning significantly outperformed those from direct prompting, as judged by both LLMs and human experts.

Armstrong's theory posits that laws of nature are contingent relations between universals, bridging constituent processes to associated laws.

Researchers introduced AIChilles, a tool that automatically finds workloads where AI-evolved programs regress in correctness, runtime, memory, or output quality compared to baselines. Across five system applications and 30 AI-evolved programs, it uncovered 49 distinct hidden weaknesses. As AI-driven system evolution becomes more common, ensuring reliability is critical; AIChilles addresses the gap in automated weakness detection, potentially preventing regressions in production. This could increase trust in AI-generated code and improve the safety of autonomous software development. AIChilles combines deterministic workload-parameter extraction, agent-based constraint inference, differential oracles, and code-frequency coverage to discover diverse failures. The paper shows that including AIChilles in the AI-driven development lifecycle can mitigate several weaknesses.

The near-optimal theoretical bound and practical integration into vLLM promise significant latency reductions for interactive LLM applications. SVF achieves a competitive ratio of 3, a dramatic improvement from the previous best of 48. This work bridges imitation learning and self-supervised learning, enabling robots to learn efficient policy representations from unlabeled data, reducing reliance on expensive expert demonstrations and improving generalization. ACT-JEPA is trained end-to-end and operates in latent space, filtering out irrelevant details to build a robust world model. It outperforms the strongest baseline across all tested environments.

This paper introduces a high-order reciprocal-based control barrier function (CBF) and a gradient similarity concept to achieve safe reinforcement learning with optimality and stability guarantees for nonlinear systems under high-relative-degree constraints and unknown disturbances. This work addresses the fundamental trade-off between safety and performance in learning-based control, providing theoretical guarantees that are crucial for safety-critical applications such as autonomous driving and robotics. The proposed high-order reciprocal CBF extends existing CBF designs to handle high-relative-degree constraints without requiring knowledge of disturbance bounds, while gradient similarity quantifies the alignment between safety and performance gradients to guide safe policy optimization.

However, standard CBFs struggle with high-relative-degree constraints (where the constraint function must be differentiated multiple times before the control input appears) and unknown disturbances. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers a framework for optimal control but often lacks safety guarantees. This paper bridges the gap by integrating CBFs into a model-based RL framework with gradient manipulation.

## [FiMi-RM: Autonomously Mitigating Length Bias in RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12843) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose FiMi-RM, a three-stage framework that autonomously learns and corrects non-linear length bias in reward models for RLHF, improving alignment without sacrificing performance. Length bias in reward models leads to reward hacking, where models favor longer but not necessarily better responses. FiMi-RM addresses this critical issue, potentially improving the quality and efficiency of LLM alignment. FiMi-RM first trains a standard biased reward model, then fits a lightweight model to capture non-linear length-reward relations, and finally decouples length from reward. Experiments show it improves length-controlled win rate and reduces verbosity in DPO and Best-of-N.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) uses a reward model trained on human preferences to guide LLM alignment. Previous mitigation methods either fail to characterize the bias or assume a simple linear relationship.

This paper introduces epistemic bias injection (EBI), a novel attack on retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems where adversaries inject factually correct but biased passages to shift LLM outputs toward a desired stance without triggering detection. EBI is more subtle than previous attacks that inject false or toxic content, as it evades fact-checking and linguistic analysis, posing a serious threat to the reliability of RAG-based LLMs used in applications like search engines and question answering. The authors propose a geometric metric to quantify stance polarity and epistemic bias, and develop a lightweight defense called BiasDef. Experiments show the attack induces significant stance shifts while evading existing defenses, and BiasDef effectively reduces adversarial retrieval and bias.

Epistemic bias injection is a more subtle variant that uses only factually correct text to bias the LLM's stance on open-ended issues.

## [Auto-exploration RL achieves optimal sample complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06244) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new class of reinforcement learning methods with auto-exploration achieves O(ε^{-2}) sample complexity for both tabular and linear function approximation settings, without requiring algorithm-dependent parameters. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in RL by eliminating the need for manually tuned exploration parameters, potentially making RL algorithms more practical and efficient for real-world applications. The method integrates auto-exploration into policy mirror descent and uses a data-driven stopping time for tabular settings, while for linear function approximation it proposes a new sampling distribution based on discounted visitation distribution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) involves an agent learning to make decisions by interacting with an environment. The exploration-exploitation dilemma is a key challenge: the agent must explore unknown actions to discover rewards while exploiting known actions to maximize cumulative reward. Tabular RL assumes small state-action spaces that can be represented as tables, while linear function approximation handles larger spaces by approximating value functions with linear combinations of features. Sample complexity measures how many interactions an algorithm needs to achieve a given performance level.

TIDAL introduces a hierarchical dual-frequency framework for diffusion-based Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models, decoupling semantic reasoning from high-frequency actuation to achieve approximately 9 Hz control updates on edge hardware, compared to approximately 2.4 Hz baselines. This work addresses a critical latency bottleneck in VLA models, enabling real-time, high-frequency control on resource-constrained edge devices, which is essential for dynamic robotic tasks like interception. It is backbone-agnostic and orthogonal to system-level optimizations, making it broadly applicable. TIDAL uses a low-frequency macro-intent loop to cache semantic embeddings and a high-frequency micro-control loop for interleaved single-step flow integration with execution. It also introduces a temporally misaligned training strategy and a differential motion predictor to handle latency shifts and velocity insensitivity.

## [Rational Activations Exponentially Outperform Standard Ones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12390) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proves that neural networks using trainable low-degree rational activation functions are exponentially more expressive and parameter-efficient than networks with standard fixed activations like ReLU, Sigmoid, or GELU. This theoretical breakthrough could reshape neural network architecture design by enabling much smaller models to achieve the same accuracy, reducing computational cost and memory usage across deep learning applications. The paper establishes an exponential gap: rational networks need only poly(log log(1/ε)) parameter overhead to approximate any function, while standard networks require Ω(log(1/ε)) parameters in the worst case. The rational activations can be seamlessly integrated into existing architectures and trained via backpropagation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Activation functions are crucial components in neural networks that introduce nonlinearity. Standard choices like ReLU, Sigmoid, and Tanh are fixed and not trainable. Rational activation functions, which are ratios of polynomials, can be learned during training and have shown promise in prior work for improving expressivity.
