ZeroWBC introduces a teleoperation-free framework that learns natural whole-body humanoid interaction from human egocentric videos paired with motion and text annotations, using a generation-then-tracking approach with a fine-tuned vision-language model. This approach eliminates the need for expensive whole-body teleoperation data, significantly reducing the cost and complexity of training humanoid robots for diverse scene-aware behaviors, and offers a scalable paradigm for learning natural humanoid interaction from readily available human egocentric data. ZeroWBC uses a fine-tuned Vision-Language Model to generate future human whole-body motion tokens from an initial egocentric image and language instruction, which are decoded and retargeted to the humanoid. Experiments on the Unitree G1 humanoid robot demonstrate diverse scene-aware behaviors without robot teleoperation demonstrations.

This work bridges the gap between text-driven motion generation and physically feasible robot execution, enabling humanoid robots to perform complex, agile motions beyond simple walking, which is critical for real-world deployment in dynamic environments. ## [DynamicPO: Solving Preference Collapse in LLM Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies a new phenomenon called 'preference optimization collapse' in LLM-based recommendation systems, where adding more negative samples degrades performance, and proposes DynamicPO, a plug-and-play framework with two adaptive mechanisms to mitigate this collapse. This work challenges the common assumption that more negative samples always improve preference optimization, providing both theoretical and empirical insights that can guide future research in recommendation systems and preference alignment. DynamicPO includes Dynamic Boundary Negative Selection to prioritize informative negatives near the decision boundary, and Dual-Margin Dynamic beta Adjustment to calibrate optimization strength per sample. Experiments on three public datasets show it prevents collapse and improves accuracy with negligible overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a method to align LLM outputs with user preferences using pairs of preferred and dispreferred responses. In recommendation systems, multiple negative samples are often used to sharpen preference boundaries, but this paper shows that too many negatives can cause gradient suppression and collapse.

## [SplitZip: Ultra-Fast Lossless KV Compression for LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01708) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SplitZip introduces a GPU-friendly lossless compression method for KV cache transfer in disaggregated LLM serving, achieving up to 613.3 GB/s compression and 2181.8 GB/s decompression throughput on BF16 tensors. This addresses a critical bottleneck in disaggregated LLM serving, where KV cache transfer between prefill and decode workers can significantly delay generation. SplitZip's speedups (up to 1.32x for transfer, 1.30x for TTFT, and 1.23x for throughput) can improve the efficiency and scalability of large-scale LLM deployments. SplitZip exploits redundancy in floating-point exponents of KV activations, encoding frequent exponent values with fixed-length codes and routing rare exponents through a sparse escape stream. It uses a calibrated top-16 exponent codebook to eliminate online histogramming, making both encoding and decoding efficient on GPUs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: In disaggregated LLM serving, the prefill phase generates a KV cache that must be transferred to decode workers, creating a network bottleneck. Existing lossless compressors are not suitable because they target offline weight compression, run on CPUs, or use variable-length coding that cannot keep up with KV production during prefill. SplitZip is designed to be GPU-friendly and integrates without modifying model execution.

## [Memini: Biologically-Inspired Continual Learning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05097) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Memini, an associative memory system for large language models that uses coupled fast and slow variables inspired by the Benna-Fusi model of synaptic consolidation to enable continual knowledge updating through episodic sensitivity, gradual consolidation, and selective forgetting. This work addresses a critical limitation of static LLMs by enabling them to adapt to a changing world without retraining, potentially reducing computational costs and improving real-world applicability. Memini organizes knowledge as a directed graph where each edge carries two coupled internal variables (fast and slow), following the Benna-Fusi model. The paper is an early-stage conceptual design from a workshop and lacks experimental validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are typically trained once and deployed in a static state, unable to update their knowledge as the world changes. Biological memory, in contrast, uses multi-timescale dynamics to form new associations quickly, strengthen repeated ones, and forget irrelevant information. The Benna-Fusi model of synaptic consolidation explains how synapses can achieve long memory lifetimes and strong initial traces through coupled variables. Memini applies this biological principle to external memory for LLMs.

## [Systematic Evaluation of Defenses Against Persistent Memory Attacks on LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08442) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper evaluates six defenses against persistent memory attacks on stateful LLM agents across four architectural layers, finding that only Memory Sandbox reduces attack success rate to 0% for most models, while others fail. This is the first systematic characterization of why each defense class fails against persistent memory attacks, providing crucial guidance for practitioners building secure LLM agents. The study conducted 5,040 runs across nine open-source models, testing defenses including input-level filtering, retrieval-level filtering, prompt hardening, and tool-gating at the memory layer. Memory Sandbox reduced ASR to 0% for eight of nine models but caused a reasoning model to invert from 0% to 100% ASR.

**Background**: Persistent memory attacks on LLM agents involve injecting malicious instructions via RAG-retrieved documents that are stored in persistent memory and executed in later sessions. These attacks exploit the agent's ability to recall past information across sessions, making them particularly dangerous for stateful agents used in real-world applications.

## [Skill-MAS: Evolving Meta-Skill for Automatic Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18837) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Skill-MAS introduces a meta-skill evolution framework that decouples experience retention from parametric updates, enabling LLM-based multi-agent systems to learn from past experience without gradient updates. This bridges the gap between inference-time and training-time multi-agent systems, allowing frontier LLMs to retain and improve orchestration strategies without fine-tuning, which could significantly enhance performance and cost-efficiency in complex task automation. Skill-MAS operates via a closed optimization loop: Multi-Trajectory Rollout samples behavioral distributions under the current Meta-Skill, and Selective Reflection applies hierarchical contrastive analysis to distill systemic experience into generalizable strategy-level principles. Experiments across four benchmarks and four LLMs show robust performance gains and strong transferability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based multi-agent systems (MAS) can tackle complex tasks by coordinating multiple agents. Existing approaches fall into two categories: inference-time MAS uses frozen frontier LLMs but cannot learn from past experience, while training-time MAS internalizes experience via gradient updates but is limited to smaller models due to high computational cost. Skill-MAS offers a third path by treating orchestration as an evolvable meta-skill that improves through experience without modifying model parameters.

## [RARM: Confidence-Gated Progress Reward Model for Robot RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22027) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Reference-Anchored Reward Model (RARM), a lightweight visual comparator that converts a single successful demonstration into dense, progress-aware rewards for reinforcement learning in robot manipulation, using a contrastive temporal objective trained on general-purpose videos. RARM addresses a key bottleneck in reinforcement learning for robot manipulation by eliminating the need for task-specific reward engineering, enabling more efficient learning on long-horizon tasks like cloth folding where sparse rewards are insufficient. RARM uses a confidence-gating mechanism to suppress uncertain matches, preventing false-positive rewards from visually plausible but physically incorrect states. It was evaluated on 9 simulated tasks from LIBERO and MetaWorld and 4 real-world tasks, achieving the best overall success rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for robot manipulation often struggles with reward design: sparse success rewards provide weak supervision, while hand-crafted dense rewards are labor-intensive and generalize poorly. Progress-based reward models estimate how far an observation has advanced toward task completion, but existing methods often require task-specific demonstrations or progress labels. RARM overcomes these limitations by learning from a single demonstration and general-purpose videos.

## [PhoneBuddy: Training Open Models for Agentic Phone Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23049) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PhoneBuddy introduces a training recipe that combines real and mock app environments to improve open models' performance on agentic phone use tasks, achieving a 45.33% task success rate on a 150-task human evaluation, up from 36.67% after supervised fine-tuning. This work addresses a key bottleneck in deploying AI agents on real devices by showing that mock environments can complement real-app reinforcement learning, making training more scalable and reproducible. It has significant implications for the development of practical phone-use agents. PhoneBuddy uses a shared supervised fine-tuning stage followed by reinforcement learning across both real and mock environments, with the mock environment PhoneWorld reconstructing runnable apps from real GUI usage structure. The gains are strongest on app and mini-app tasks, while cross-app workflows remain challenging.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Training AI agents to use phones is difficult because real devices are slow, stateful, and hard to reset, while mock environments only approximate real behavior. PhoneBuddy combines both to leverage their complementary strengths, using a mock environment called PhoneWorld that builds runnable apps from real usage traces.

## [Memory Contagion: Bias Spreads Across Time in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23195) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Memory Contagion, a phenomenon where biased evaluator feedback propagates through LLM agent memory systems across time, affecting future agents even with perfect memory consolidation. This reveals a critical vulnerability in current agent memory designs, as biases can persist and spread across generations of agents, impacting AI safety and reliability in multi-agent systems. The study tested length bias and authority bias across four experimental phases, finding that length bias propagates even at contamination rates as low as p=0.2, while authority bias did not propagate in any experiment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents use memory systems to maintain long-term coherence, but prior work assumed memories come from unbiased experiences. This paper shows that biased evaluator feedback can be stored and later retrieved by other agents, causing cross-temporal bias propagation.

## [Knowledge Editing in LLMs Is an Illusion, Not Erasure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23276) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that knowledge editing in LLMs does not truly erase facts but merely suppresses them, as edited knowledge persists in narrow, sensitive regions of the model's representation space. This challenges the reliability of knowledge editing for model safety and trustworthiness, showing that edited facts can be recovered via adversarial prompts, undermining current deployment practices. The paper uses adversarial elicitation and mechanistic analysis to show that low-rank updates redistribute rather than overwrite knowledge, and that edited knowledge lies in anisotropic loss landscapes vulnerable to perturbations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge editing aims to update specific facts in LLMs without retraining, but its effectiveness has been debated. Mechanistic interpretability seeks to reverse-engineer neural network internals. This work combines both to expose fundamental vulnerabilities.

## [Sol Video Inference Engine: Agentic Acceleration for Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23743) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Sol Video Inference Engine is a training-free, agent-native framework that automatically optimizes video diffusion model inference by combining five acceleration techniques—cache, sparse attention, token pruning, quantization, and kernel fusion—tailored to specific model, hardware, and configuration combinations. This framework addresses the critical challenge of instance-specific acceleration in video generation, where the optimal strategy varies across models, hardware, and settings, potentially reducing inference cost by over 2x while maintaining quality, which is vital for practical deployment of large video diffusion models. The framework was validated on three models of varying sizes: 64B Cosmos3-Super, 22B LTX-2.3, and 2B SANA-Video, achieving over 2x end-to-end acceleration with near-lossless VBench quality. It uses parallel skill agents for each technique, an agent integrator to compose them, and a human validator for quality feedback.

**Background**: Video diffusion models generate high-quality videos but suffer from high inference cost due to iterative denoising. Many acceleration methods exist, but their effectiveness is instance-specific—depending on model architecture, hardware, and inference settings—making manual tuning impractical. The Sol framework automates this tuning process using an agentic approach.

## [Reinforcement Learning Tunes LHC Triggers on Real Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23993) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers applied reinforcement learning to dynamically adjust trigger thresholds at the Large Hadron Collider, achieving a 56% improvement in in-tolerance time intervals on real collision data (CMS Run 283408) without fine-tuning. This is the first demonstration of RL-based trigger control on real LHC data, showing potential to significantly improve signal efficiency and adapt to changing conditions, which could enhance physics discovery potential at high-energy colliders. The study adapted Group-Filtered Policy Optimization (GFPO) to streaming control and introduced two variants (GFPO-F, GFPO-FR) that enforce background rate feasibility during training. On Monte Carlo streams, the agent increased in-tolerance intervals by 48% for H_T trigger and 28% for anomaly-detection trigger.

**Background**: The Large Hadron Collider (LHC) at CERN produces enormous amounts of collision data, but only a fraction can be stored due to bandwidth and storage limits. Trigger systems perform real-time event filtering to select potentially interesting events. Traditionally, trigger thresholds are hand-tuned and static, becoming suboptimal as detector conditions change over time.

## [AutoSpec: Evolving LLM Agent Safety Rules via ILP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24245) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoSpec is a framework that automatically evolves expert-designed safety rules for LLM agents using inductive logic programming (ILP) guided by counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS). It iteratively refines rules from user annotations, achieving high F1 scores (0.98 and 0.93) across code execution and embodied agent domains. This work addresses the critical tradeoff between interpretability and accuracy in LLM agent safety, offering a method that produces human-readable, auditable rules while significantly reducing false positives (up to 94%) and maintaining high recall. It could enable safer deployment of autonomous LLM agents in sensitive applications. AutoSpec uses ILP to efficiently prune the exponential search space of rule edits by identifying predicates that discriminate false positives from false negatives. It converges within 4-5 iterations and achieves up to 4.8x higher F1 than heuristic CEGIS on 291 execution traces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Inductive logic programming (ILP) is a symbolic AI technique that learns logic programs from examples and background knowledge. Counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS) iteratively generates candidate solutions and refines them using counterexamples. LLM agents combine language models with external tools, posing safety risks that existing hand-crafted rules or neural classifiers handle poorly due to brittleness or lack of interpretability.

Researchers propose COMAD, a framework for continual offline multi-agent skill discovery that uses skill partition and reuse to mitigate catastrophic forgetting and plasticity loss in sequential tasks. This addresses a critical gap in multi-agent continual learning, enabling agents to accumulate and reuse coordination skills over time without forgetting, which is essential for real-world applications like robotics and autonomous driving. This paper introduces signed pattern control for variable-order Markov generation using belief propagation, allowing attraction or repulsion of target patterns in sequences via positive or negative coupling. This method addresses the problem of recurring high-order 'tunnels' in long sequence generation, with potential applications in computational creativity, music generation, and any domain requiring controlled diversity. The method uses a weighted recurrence automaton to compute an activation R for target patterns, and belief propagation samples from a distribution that rewards or penalizes those patterns. Experiments on Bach, Telemann, and jazz solos show reduced self-reuse and increased coverage.

## [Retrieval-Augmented Personalization for Wearable Stress Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24985) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a retrieval-augmented personalization method that uses frozen foundation models to improve wearable stress detection accuracy by +3.92% and macro F1-score by +4.76% on the WESAD dataset, without requiring any labeled user data or fine-tuning. This approach addresses the key challenge of inter-individual variability in stress detection, offering a lightweight and privacy-preserving alternative to costly fine-tuning, which could accelerate the deployment of personalized health monitoring in wearables. The method retrieves similar patterns from a target user's history using frozen out-of-domain foundation models, encodes them into a compact personalized embedding, and modulates a lightweight transformer network. Temporal retrieval with only prior user samples achieves performance close to full intra-user retrieval, demonstrating robustness to limited user history.

A new study reveals that multi-sensor fusion models for cattle posture classification achieve high accuracy in controlled settings but fail dramatically under animal-level and temporal distribution shifts, with macro-F1 dropping from 0.94 to 0.49 in cross-year evaluation. This finding challenges the common assumption that adding more sensors always improves robustness, and highlights the critical need for rigorous distribution-shift testing in precision agriculture and other sensor-based ML systems. The study used collar accelerometers, rumen-bolus sensors, and environmental data from beef cattle across two years, with XGBoost as the primary model. Explainability analysis showed persistent reliance on rumen-bolus activity even when performance deteriorated.

## [Uncertainty-Aware RL for Chemical Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24990) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes two complementary methods to incorporate predictive uncertainty into reinforcement learning for chemical language models, enabling more robust exploration of chemical space and increasing true hit rate by 0.25 (from 0.5 to 0.75). By addressing the neglect of uncertainty in existing RL frameworks for molecular design, this work improves the reliability of hit discovery and reduces exploration of poorly supported chemical regions, which is crucial for practical drug discovery. The two methods are: (1) treating uncertainty as an additional optimization objective, and (2) using uncertainty to modulate policy updates. They were evaluated on a controlled model system, ChemProp models, and a conformal prediction wrapper on a random forest classifier.

This paper introduces a benchmark to evaluate the robustness of tabular foundation models (TFMs) to biologically inspired distribution shifts in microbiome abundance data, finding that protecting discriminative features is insufficient for stability. The findings challenge common assumptions about feature preservation and highlight sensitivity to zero-inflation shifts. The benchmark uses six gut microbiome datasets across four disease contexts, applying three perturbation strategies: removal of high-abundance taxa, sparsification via increased zero-inflation, and zero-imputation via spurious non-zero injections. Zero-imputation consistently caused the most harm, and sparsification disproportionately affected TFMs compared to random forest baselines.

## [ADOWIP: Budgeted Decision-Loss Priority for Online Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25068) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ADOWIP, a framework with a decision-loss priority gate that selectively updates online time-series forecasters only when beneficial, given delayed labels and compute budgets. It provides theoretical guarantees and empirical validation on public datasets. This work addresses the practical question of when to update rather than how, which is crucial for deploying online learning systems under limited compute budgets. It could improve efficiency and performance in real-world forecasting tasks like capacity planning and bike rebalancing. ADOWIP uses sealed delay queues, exact budget accounting, and an observed decision-loss priority gate that updates only when downstream loss exceeds a calibrated empirical quantile. On ETT capacity-planning tasks, it outperforms always-update, fixed-period, and drift-triggered baselines under matched compute.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Online time-series forecasting often faces delayed labels and limited compute budgets. Traditional approaches update models at every opportunity, which can waste resources and even degrade performance. ADOWIP introduces a principled way to decide when to update based on decision loss, balancing adaptation benefits against compute costs.

## [GCT-MARL: Graph Contrastive Transfer for Multi-Agent RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25073) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GCT-MARL introduces a graph-based contrastive transfer learning framework that augments the multi-view graph contrastive backbone of MAIL with an adaptively weighted alignment loss and a two-phase training protocol, enabling efficient transfer across varying team sizes and compositions in cooperative multi-agent reinforcement learning. This framework significantly improves sample efficiency and convergence speed in cooperative MARL, reducing the need to train agents from scratch for each new environment, which is crucial for real-world deployment. It also naturally supports continual learning, making it a unified approach to address key limitations in current MARL transfer methods. The framework is validated empirically in both homogeneous (within-faction, varying N) and heterogeneous (cross-faction and mixed unit-type) transfer scenarios, showing marked acceleration over from-scratch training. It also demonstrates continual learning by sequentially chaining the two-phase transfer protocol across related tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) trains multiple agents to cooperate or compete in shared environments. Transfer learning aims to reuse knowledge from previous tasks to accelerate learning on new tasks, but existing MARL transfer methods often struggle with varying team sizes and compositions. Graph contrastive learning leverages graph structures to learn robust representations by contrasting different views of the same data. MAIL (Multi-Agent Imitation Learning) is a prior framework that uses multi-view graph contrastive learning for MARL, which GCT-MARL builds upon.

BCoughBench evaluates five respiratory acoustic foundation models on nine classification and three regression tasks under five body-coupled wearable sensor conditions, finding mean AUROC drops from 0.785 (smartphone) to 0.689–0.723. This study highlights a critical gap in respiratory acoustic AI: models trained on smartphone recordings perform poorly on body-coupled wearables, which are increasingly used in clinical settings. The findings urge the development of sensor-robust models for reliable deployment. No foundation model meets the clinical sensitivity threshold (Se@Sp95 ≥ 0.20) on most disease tasks under any body-coupled sensor. Sex classification on the CIDRZ cohort collapses (AUROC from 0.954 to 0.596–0.628), while COVID detection is nearly unaffected (Δ = -0.004).

## [Memory Roles in RAG Conversational Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25361) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a fine-grained taxonomy of conversational memory and a user-centric evaluation framework to analyze how different memory roles affect response quality in RAG-based conversational agents. This work addresses the underexplored question of how memory functional roles shape agent responses, potentially improving personalization and response quality in conversational AI systems. The taxonomy classifies retrieved memories into distinct role types, and the evaluation framework simulates user perspectives. Experiments on long-term datasets with frontier LLMs show that clarifying memory improves factual accuracy and constraint awareness, while irrelevant memory degrades topic relevance.

Existing evaluations are often reference-based and miss user-centric nuances.

## [Conformal Recovery-Deadline Certificates for Adaptive Controllers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25371) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce conformal recovery-deadline certificates, a distribution-free, finite-sample upper bound on the recovery time of adapting controllers, enabling delayed fallback in runtime assurance with coverage guarantees. This addresses a key limitation of standard latching shields that prematurely trip on recovery transients, potentially improving safety and autonomy in safety-critical systems like spacecraft and robotics. The method uses split conformal prediction to certify deadlines, separates autonomy (statistical coverage) from safety (verified backstop), and is demonstrated on a 6-DOF spacecraft attitude controller and a torque-controlled inverted pendulum.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime assurance (RTA) protects safety-critical systems by switching to a verified safe controller when a violation is detected. Standard latching shields trip on the first breach, which is problematic for adaptive controllers that need a bounded recovery transient to identify and correct faults. Conformal prediction provides distribution-free uncertainty quantification with finite-sample guarantees.

This paper formulates the problem of finding minimal arithmetic circuits for polynomials as a reinforcement learning task, introducing FactorLibrary to store reusable subgoals. The top-down PPO+MCTS agent achieves a 91.8% success rate for circuits up to complexity 8. This work bridges reinforcement learning and algebraic complexity, offering a fresh approach to a hard combinatorial problem. The reusable subgoal mechanism could inspire more efficient search in other structured optimization domains. The paper compares bottom-up (Gumbel-PPO-MCTS) and top-down (PPO+MCTS, SAC) agents, with top-down PPO+MCTS showing the most stable performance. FactorLibrary stores factorizable subexpressions that serve as reusable subgoals across episodes.

## [TopoCast: Topological Fidelity for Time Series Forecasts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25439) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TopoCast, a topology-driven framework that uses Takens delay embedding and persistent homology to evaluate structural fidelity in time series forecasting, introducing the Topological Fidelity Score (TFS) and Localized Topological Fidelity Score (LTFS). Traditional pointwise error metrics like MSE overlook structural issues such as phase shifts and over-smoothing, so TopoCast provides a more comprehensive evaluation that can reveal hidden failure modes in Transformer-based forecasting models. TopoCast reconstructs phase-space representations via Takens delay embedding and applies persistent homology to derive four topological fidelity measures, which are aggregated into TFS; LTFS further adds temporal localization by mapping persistent features to the time domain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting models are often evaluated using pointwise error metrics like MSE, which measure numerical accuracy but ignore structural properties such as recurrent dynamics and phase alignment. Takens delay embedding is a technique from nonlinear dynamics that reconstructs a system's phase space from a single time series, while persistent homology is a topological data analysis method that quantifies the evolution of topological features across scales.

## [SR-PPO: Single-Rollout PPO with Token-Level Credit Assignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25451) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose SR-PPO, a variant of Proximal Policy Optimization that uses a single rollout per prompt and a Monte Carlo Pass@k critic for token-level credit assignment, reducing computational cost and improving credit attribution in reinforcement learning for language models. This work addresses a key challenge in reinforcement learning for language models: efficiently assigning credit to individual tokens without expensive repeated sampling. If validated, SR-PPO could enable more efficient training of reasoning models, reducing the computational overhead of methods like GRPO. SR-PPO trains a token-level critic to predict Pass@k success probability from a single rollout, using Pass@1 as a base. As k increases, Pass@k approximates a reachability indicator, and in an explicit state graph, the limit can be computed in O(|V|+|E|) time. Initial experiments show stable learning and improved Pass@128 rates on math benchmarks HMMT26 and AIME24.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for language models often uses algorithms like PPO or GRPO, which require multiple rollouts per prompt to estimate advantages. Token-level credit assignment is difficult because outcome rewards are sparse and trajectories are heterogeneous. SR-PPO aims to overcome these limitations by using a single rollout and a learned critic.

## [Red Teaming Framework for LLM Faithfulness Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25476) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new red teaming framework using target, attacker, and jury models systematically uncovers LLM vulnerabilities, achieving up to 7.9% higher attack success in faithfulness evaluation on question-answering tasks. This framework provides a scalable methodology for ongoing safety evaluation of LLMs, addressing critical reliability concerns in high-stakes applications. It also reveals that architectural design choices often outweigh parameter scaling in determining model safety. The framework is adaptable across tasks and languages, from English question-answering to Arabic summarization, but faces challenges in fully automating adversarial prompt generation across languages. It also has limitations in detecting subtle unfaithfulness that does not manifest as explicit factual contradictions.

**Background**: LLM red teaming is the process of detecting vulnerabilities such as bias, PII leakage, or misinformation through intentionally adversarial prompts. This work addresses two critical challenges in reconstruction-based 3D anomaly detection: detecting weak defects like scratches and reducing false positives from background positional bias, which can significantly improve quality control in high-precision manufacturing. PCDiff achieves high-quality generation of weak-defective anomalies by conditioning on texture gradient, image patch, text, and mask, and ensures local anomaly restoration and global geometric consistency through a joint local-global reconstruction algorithm.

The framework treats the differentiable waveguide method and WGNO as end-to-end physics engines, recovering absorber permittivity via automatic differentiation. ## [Majority Reasoning Not Finitely Axiomatizable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proves that strict majority reasoning in finite social decision frames cannot be captured by any bounded finite fragment of a coherence criterion, resolving a conjecture by Moss and Pedersen (2026). This result establishes a fundamental limitation on the finite axiomatizability of majority reasoning in judgment aggregation, deepening our understanding of the logical foundations of social choice theory. For every k≥1, the authors construct a maximal standard frame whose shortest coherence violation has length exactly 2k+2, showing no uniform finite bound on the incoherence index exists. The construction is geometric, using orthogonality and dimension in rational vector spaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Judgment aggregation studies how to combine individual judgments on logically connected propositions into a collective judgment. A coherence criterion characterizes when majority judgments can be represented by a finitely additive measure. Finite axiomatizability asks whether a finite set of axioms can capture all valid reasoning patterns.

A new paper titled 'The Token Not Taken: Sampling, State, and the Stochasticity of AI Agents' systematically analyzes sources of variability in AI agent behavior, distinguishing intrinsic token sampling from extrinsic factors like changing environments and serving infrastructure. This work clarifies a fundamental yet often conflated source of unpredictability in agentic AI, which has practical implications for debugging, reliability, and deterministic control in deployed systems. The paper focuses on autoregressive token generation where small differences in sampled tokens can propagate into different tool calls, code paths, or agent states. It also notes that deterministic execution does not guarantee identical behavior in deployed settings due to extrinsic factors.

**Background**: AI agents often use large foundation models that generate text token by token. During generation, the model computes probabilities for the next token, and a decoder may sample from these probabilities using a pseudo-random number generator, introducing intrinsic stochasticity. This variability can compound through the agent's orchestration loop, leading to different outcomes for the same input.

TOTEN is a knowledge-based system that preserves physical quantities and technical notation as intact entities in Brazilian Portuguese text, avoiding fragmentation issues from BPE tokenization. It uses a formal ontology of engineering entities (OEE) and deterministic coupling to external authorities like Pint, Unicode Character Database, and RSLP. This addresses a critical problem in NLP for technical text, where tokenization fragmentation of physical quantities causes errors downstream. TOTEN's approach could improve the accuracy of AI systems that reason quantitatively over technical documents, especially in Brazilian Portuguese. TOTEN achieves unit atomicity in all contrasts and numerical reconstruction of 0.775-0.904 externally vs. 0.627-0.703 for the best baseline (Quantulum3). On the internal EngQuant benchmark, it scores 0.780 vs. 0.340, with statistically significant differences (McNemar, Holm-corrected).

**Background**: Byte-Pair Encoding (BPE) is a tokenization algorithm used in many LLMs that compresses text by merging frequent byte pairs, but it can fragment physical quantities like '10 m/s' into arbitrary subwords. This fragmentation is problematic for technical text where preserving the integrity of numbers, units, and symbols is crucial for downstream reasoning. TOTEN addresses this by using a knowledge-based approach with a formal ontology and external libraries for dimensional analysis and morphology.

DiPO achieves state-of-the-art forget quality on the TOFU benchmark while maintaining strong model utility, offering a more generalizable and effective solution for LLM unlearning, which is critical for data privacy and AI safety. DiPO constructs preference distribution pairs by selectively amplifying or suppressing the model's high-confidence output logits, eliminating the need for domain-specific knowledge or well-designed prompts. The method is theoretically proven to be consistent with the desired unlearning direction.

Researchers introduced STGAT, a spatio-temporal graph attention network that detects clock drift, time-synchronization manipulation, and Y2K38 Unix overflow in energy IoT systems, achieving 95.7% accuracy and 26% faster detection. This work addresses a critical and growing threat to energy cyber-physical systems, where timestamp integrity is essential for reliable operation. STGAT's ability to detect both gradual drift and catastrophic overflow events like Y2K38 could prevent cascading failures in smart grids and microgrids. STGAT combines drift-aware temporal embeddings and temporal self-attention to model individual device time evolution, and uses graph attention to capture spatial propagation of timing errors. A curvature-regularized latent representation separates normal clock behavior from anomalies, and experiments show significant improvements over baselines (Cohen's d > 1.8, p < 0.001).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Energy IoT devices like smart meters rely on accurate timestamps for coordination and logging. However, these devices often suffer from clock drift due to hardware imperfections, and the Y2K38 problem occurs when 32-bit Unix timestamps overflow on January 19, 2038, causing systems to misinterpret dates. Traditional anomaly detection assumes timestamps are reliable, making them blind to temporal inconsistencies.

## [ReaDy-Go: Real-to-Sim Dynamic 3D GS for Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11575) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReaDy-Go introduces a real-to-sim pipeline that uses dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting to create photorealistic training environments with moving human obstacles, enabling robust visual navigation policies. The system outperforms baselines in both simulation and real-world experiments, and demonstrates zero-shot sim-to-real transfer to unseen environments. This work addresses the sim-to-real gap in visual navigation by generating dynamic, photorealistic training data specific to target deployment environments, which is critical for safe robot navigation in real-world dynamic settings. It advances prior static-only 3D Gaussian Splatting methods and opens new possibilities for environment-specific policy training. The pipeline includes a dynamic GS simulator that integrates static scene GS with a human animation module, enabling insertion of animatable human GS avatars and synthesis of plausible motions from 2D trajectories. It also provides a navigation dataset generation framework using a robot expert planner designed for dynamic GS representations and a human planner.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Visual navigation models often fail in real-world dynamic environments due to the sim-to-real gap, where training in simulation does not transfer well to reality. 3D Gaussian Splatting (GS) is a recent technique for photorealistic 3D scene reconstruction and rendering from multiple images, but prior GS-based navigation works only considered static scenes or non-photorealistic obstacles. ReaDy-Go extends GS to dynamic scenes with photorealistic human obstacles, enabling more realistic training for navigation policies.

## [Logit Distance Bounds Representational Similarity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15438) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proves that closeness in logit distance between discriminative models guarantees high linear representational similarity, addressing a limitation of KL divergence. The authors define a representational dissimilarity measure and show it is bounded by logit distance. This result provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for comparing internal representations of neural networks, which is crucial for interpretability and AI safety. It shows that logit-distance distillation preserves linear-probe recoverability of concepts better than KL-based distillation. The bound holds for a broad family of discriminative models including autoregressive language models. The paper also shows that KL divergence upper-bounds logit distance only when model probabilities are bounded away from zero, which is often not satisfied in practice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Identifiability results show that if two discriminative models induce the same conditional distributions, their internal representations are equal up to an invertible linear transformation. However, prior work by Nielsen et al. (2025) found that closeness in KL divergence does not imply high linear representational similarity. Logit distance measures the difference in log-odds (logits) between model outputs, which is more directly tied to the model's internal geometry.

This paper systematically evaluates tiny-to-small language models (135M to 7B parameters) for semantic reasoning in AI-native 6G networks using the standardization-aligned 6G-Bench benchmark, finding that mid-scale models (1.5B to 3B) offer the best balance of accuracy and efficiency. This research provides deployment-relevant guidance for 6G standardization efforts, showing that smaller models can achieve practical reasoning without the computational cost of frontier LLMs, which is critical for edge-native infrastructures. Accuracy increases from 0.224 (135M) to 0.707 (7B), but gains are non-uniform; a stability transition occurs around 1-1.5B parameters. The Edge Score metric normalizes accuracy by latency and memory footprint, revealing that mid-scale models (1.5-3B) achieve the most favorable balance.

## [Position Graphs: A New Framework for Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25719) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces position graphs, a graph-based framework that uses two strict partial orders to model relative positions of discrete tokens, and proves that structural pattern discovery in these graphs is NP-complete. This work provides a formal logical layer for document processing and other applications requiring spatial reasoning, independent of specific data extraction techniques. The NP-completeness result highlights the inherent difficulty of pattern discovery in such structured graphs. Position graphs are constrained by a chain condition and compatibility requirements focusing on rows and columns, distinguishing them from general qualitative spatial calculi. The paper establishes conditions for graph consistency and proves that induced subgraph isomorphism remains NP-complete even for this restricted class.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Qualitative spatial reasoning (QSR) deals with symbolic representation of spatial knowledge, often using relations like 'left of' or 'above'. Position graphs formalize such relations using partial orders, providing a graph-theoretic approach. The induced subgraph isomorphism problem asks whether a pattern graph appears as an induced subgraph of a larger graph, and is known to be NP-complete in general.

## [CLEF HIPE-2026: Person-Place Relation Extraction from Historical Texts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17663) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HIPE-2026 is a new CLEF evaluation lab that extends the HIPE series to semantic relation extraction, specifically person-place relations from multilingual historical texts, introducing a three-fold evaluation profile that jointly assesses accuracy, computational efficiency, and domain generalization. This lab addresses the need for efficient and generalizable relation extraction in digital humanities, enabling large-scale knowledge graph construction, historical biography reconstruction, and spatial analysis from noisy historical documents. Systems must classify two relation types: 'at' (person ever at place) and 'isAt' (person located at place around publication time), requiring reasoning over temporal and geographical cues. The lab covers four languages and uses a document-level classification task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: CLEF (Conference and Labs of the Evaluation Forum) is an organization that promotes research in multilingual information access through benchmark challenges. The HIPE (Identifying Historical People, Places and other Entities) shared task series began in 2020, focusing on named entity recognition in historical newspapers. HIPE-2026 builds on previous campaigns to target semantic relation extraction, which involves identifying relationships between entities mentioned in text.

## [HiT-JEPA: Hierarchical Self-Supervised Trajectory Embedding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00028) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose HiT-JEPA, a hierarchical self-supervised framework that learns multi-scale urban trajectory embeddings by capturing point-level details, intermediate patterns, and high-level abstractions in a three-layer hierarchy. This work addresses a key limitation in existing trajectory representation methods by integrating both local dynamics and global semantics in a single model, enabling richer similarity computation for urban computing applications. HiT-JEPA uses a Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) with VICReg regularization, and its three-layer hierarchy processes point-level, sub-trajectory, and global abstractions. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate improved trajectory similarity computation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) trains models using data itself to generate supervisory signals, without external labels. Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) is an SSL method that predicts abstract embeddings rather than reconstructing pixels. Urban trajectory embedding converts movement data into vector representations for tasks like similarity computation.

## [Hybrid TGN-SEAL Boosts Dynamic Link Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14239) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a hybrid TGN-SEAL model that integrates enclosing subgraphs into Temporal Graph Networks (TGNs) to jointly learn structural and temporal information for dynamic graph link prediction. This approach improves average precision by at least 2% over standard TGNs on sparse real-world datasets, addressing key challenges like data sparsity and temporal dependency in dynamic networks. The model extracts enclosing subgraphs around candidate links and uses a GNN to learn structural features, which are then combined with TGN's temporal embeddings. Experiments were conducted on CDR, email, and message datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Link prediction in dynamic graphs is crucial for applications like social networks and telecommunications. Traditional methods often fail to capture temporal dynamics, while TGNs model evolving node embeddings but struggle with sparse data. SEAL (Subgraph Embeddings and Attributes for Link prediction) uses enclosing subgraphs to capture local topology, and this work combines both approaches.

## [Grid-Size-Invariant Surrogate Models for Rock-Fluid Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22188) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper develops eight surrogate models for predicting fluid flow in porous media, including four grid-size-invariant neural networks that can infer on larger computational domains than those used during training. These models significantly reduce computational costs for multi-query problems like uncertainty quantification and optimization in geoscience, enabling faster and more scalable simulations. The study compares UNet and UNet++ architectures, showing that UNet++ outperforms UNet for surrogate modeling, and demonstrates that the grid-size-invariant approach reduces memory consumption during training while maintaining good accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Modeling rock-fluid interaction involves solving partial differential equations (PDEs) to predict flow and chemical reactions. High-fidelity numerical models require fine grids and are computationally expensive, limiting their use in multi-query scenarios. Surrogate models offer a cheaper alternative by learning from high-fidelity simulations.

<a id="item-299"></a>
## [Modality Gating Behavior Is Backbone-Conditional in Prostate MRI Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10702) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study systematically evaluates modality gating in multi-modal prostate MRI segmentation across two backbones (nnU-Net and Mamba), finding that gating effectiveness is backbone-conditional: it degrades nnU-Net but improves Mamba when combined with modality dropout. These findings challenge the common assumption that learned modality gates universally perform per-sample quality routing, guiding future multi-modal fusion designs to consider backbone choice. The study used 5-fold cross-validation with 180 trained models, plus gate-weight and counterfactual analysis on 30 gating models, revealing that nnU-Net gates collapse to a static prior (SD 0.0033) while Mamba gates retain sample-dependent variation (SD 0.0365).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-modal fusion in medical imaging often uses learned modality gating to weight contributions from different MRI sequences. nnU-Net is a widely used convolutional segmentation framework, while Mamba is a recent state-space model architecture. This study tests whether gating actually performs per-sample quality routing as assumed.

## [Bathroom-Centered Digital Twin for Elderly Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23292) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a bathroom-centered human-building digital twin framework that integrates human motion and environmental semantics to analyze older adults' safety during bathroom use. This framework addresses a critical gap by coupling human behavior and environmental context, enabling interaction-aware safety analysis that could reduce fall risks for aging populations. The framework includes semantic bathroom representation, skeleton-based human representation, spatial-semantic coupling, and interaction-aware event analytics, with a Unity-based proof-of-concept prototype.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 25, 04:00

**Background**: A digital twin is a virtual replica of a physical system used for simulation and monitoring. Existing bathroom safety studies often treat environment and human motion separately, limiting understanding of how older adults interact with fixtures and surfaces.
